2025-06-16T21:37:15.8144181Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8144303Z <pr_title>
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8144853Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8145493Z </pr_title>
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8145606Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8145692Z <pr_description>
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8146454Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8147542Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8147631Z ## Problem
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8147952Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8148353Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8148612Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8148879Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8149146Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8149413Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8149636Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8149752Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8150468Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8151040Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8151119Z ## Solution
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8151227Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8151323Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8151649Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8152062Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8152462Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8153313Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8153974Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8154285Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8154385Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8154670Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8154855Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8155182Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8155410Z ```bash
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8155944Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8156533Z ```
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8156631Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8156720Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8156951Z ```bash
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8157619Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8158402Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8158634Z ```
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8158727Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8158872Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8159108Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8159200Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8159770Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8160280Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8160358Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8160601Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8161080Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8161586Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8162062Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8162630Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8162935Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8163307Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8163776Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8163852Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8163955Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8163959Z

2025-06-16T21:37:15.8332834Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8333314Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8333417Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8333615Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8333696Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8334177Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8334716Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8335295Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8335841Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8336179Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8336649Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8337074Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8337154Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8337423Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8337754Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8338064Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8338279Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8338374Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8339351Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8340259Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8340340Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8341028Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8341700Z

2025-06-16T21:37:15.8347102Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8347378Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8347757Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8347844Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8347979Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8348200Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8348512Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8348585Z ```json
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8348750Z {
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8348916Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8349122Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8349372Z   },
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8349531Z   "acls": [
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8349706Z     {
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8349879Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8350092Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8350299Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8350494Z     }
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8350651Z   ]
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8350811Z }
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8350965Z ```
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8351053Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8351138Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8351269Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8351552Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8352047Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8352543Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8352999Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8353136Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8353222Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8353381Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8353845Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8354396Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8354608Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8355300Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8355733Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8356046Z 
2025-06-16T21:37:15.8356573Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.
